Canonical formulation of the plasmon – hard particle scattering process in a quark-gluon plasma
Yu.A. Markov, M.A. Markova

Abstract




Abstract

It is shown that the Hamiltonian formalism proposed previously in
[
1
]
to describe the nonlinear dynamics of only
soft
fermionic and bosonic excitations contains much more information than initially assumed. In this paper, we have demonstrated in detail that it also proved to be very appropriate and powerful in describing a wide range of other physical phenomena, including the scattering of colorless plasmons off
hard
thermal (or external) color-charged particles moving in a hot quark-gluon plasma. A generalization of the Poisson superbracket including both anticommuting variables for hard modes and normal variables of the soft Bose field, is presented for the case of continuous medium. The corresponding Hamilton equations are defined, and the most general form of the third- and fourth-order interaction Hamiltonians is written out in terms of the normal boson field variables and hard momentum modes of the quark-gluon plasma. The canonical transformations involving both bosonic and hard mode degrees of freedom of the system under consideration, are discussed. The canonicity conditions for these transformations based on the Poisson superbracket, are derived. The most general structure of canonical transformations in the form of integro-power series up to sixth order in a new normal field variable and a new hard mode variable, is presented. For the hard momentum mode of quark-gluon plasma excitations, an ansatz separating the color and momentum degrees of freedom, is proposed. The question of approximation of the total effective scattering amplitude when the momenta of hard excitations are much larger than those of soft excitations of the plasma, is considered. The self-consistent system of Boltzmann-type kinetic equations taking into account the time evolution of the mean value of the color charge of the hard particle is obtained.

1
Introduction

The present work is a direct continuation of our earlier work
[
1
]
, where we have developed in detail the approach in the construction of the Hamiltonian formalism for the self-consistent description of the nonlinear scattering processes of soft collective excitations of both bosonic and fermionic types in the QGP. The use of the methods and of the results we received in
[
1
]
allowed us to develop a somewhat different, more rigorous, as we think, approach to the problem posed in
[
2
]
. Making use of just the same initial equations and relations (canonicity conditions, Poisson’s superbracket, Hamilton’s equations) written out in
[
1
]
for soft collective modes of QGP excitations, we show step by step how one can derive from them the equations and relations describing qualitatively new physical phenomena and interaction processes. This, in turn, gives a deeper understanding of the kinetic equations themselves for soft bosonic and fermionic excitations obtained in
[
2
]
and the possibility of using them to describe the hard momentum degrees of freedom of QGP.

It should be noted at once that the kinetic equations and the equation of evolution of the color charge of a hard particle, which we derive in the present paper, do not coincide literally with the equations of the paper
[
2
]
. In the current approach, new terms appear that sometimes qualitatively change the dynamics of the evolution of physical quantities. Moreover, in contrast to the results in
[
2
]
, which are valid for arbitrary color group
SU(N_{c})
, here for a self-consistent description it is necessary to be restricted to the value
N_{c}=3
(not considering the ‘‘trivial’’ case
N_{c}=2
).

The paper is organized as follows. In section
2
, a generalization of the Poisson superbracket including both the anticommuting variables for hard modes
(\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}},\,\xi^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}})
and the normal variables
(a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}},\,a^{\,{\ast}\,a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}})
for soft boson field to the case of a continuous medium is performed. The corresponding Hamilton equations
are defined and the general structure of the third- and fourth-order interaction Hamiltonians in the normal field variables and in the hard modes of the hot quark-gluon plasma, is written out. In section
3
, the canonical transformations including bosonic and hard mode degrees of freedom of the quark-gluon plasma are discussed. Two systems of canonicity conditions for these transformations, based on the Poisson superbracket are derived. The most general structure of canonical transformations in the form of integro-power series in the new normal field variables
(c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}},\,c^{\,\ast\,a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}})
and new hard momentum mode variables
(\zeta^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}},\,\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}})
up to the terms of sixth order is presented.

In section
4
, the problem of removing the “non-essential” third-order Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
is addressed. Explicit expressions for the coefficient functions in quadratic terms in
c^{\!\phantom{\ast}a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}
and
\zeta^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}}
of canonical transformations, are obtained. An explicit form of the complete effective amplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
describing the elastic scattering process of plasmon off a hard color particle in leading tree-level order is given and the corresponding effective fourth-order Hamiltonian
{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\hskip 0.56917pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.56917ptg\hskip 0.56917ptG}
, is written out. Section
5
is concerned with the calculation of fourth- and sixth-order correlation functions in the new normal field variable
c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}
and the new hard mode variable
\zeta^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}}
. The notions of the plasmon number density
{\mathcal{N}}^{\;a\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
​, and of the number density of hard modes
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}}

are introduced. For the hard momentum modes of quark-gluon plasma excitations, we suggest an ansatz that separates the color and momentum degrees of freedom. On the basis of Hamilton’s equations of motion with the Poisson superbracket, a differential equation to which the fourth-order correlation function obeys, is defined.

In section
6
an approximate solution to the equation for the fourth-order correlator, accounting for the deviation of the four-point correlation function from the Gaussian approximation at a low level of nonlinearity in interacting Bose-excitations is found. On the basis of this solution, a matrix kinetic equation for the number density of color plasmons describing the elastic scattering process of collective gluon excitations off a hard color-charged particle, is constructed. In section
7
the question of approximation of effective subamplitudes
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},{\bf k},{\bf k}_{1}}
and
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},{\bf k},{\bf k}_{1}}
in the limit when the momenta of the hard excitations are much larger than the momenta of the soft plasma excitations, is considered. An approximate expression for the effective amplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},{\bf k},{\bf k}_{1}}
is derived and a simple graphical interpretation of the individual terms in the effective amplitude, is provided.

In section
8
we consider an approximation of the matrix kinetic equation for soft gluon excitations in the limit of large hard excitation momenta. The color decomposition of the matrix function
{\mathcal{N}}^{\;a\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
is written out and the first moment about color of the matrix kinetic equation defining a scalar kinetic equation for the colorless part
N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
of this decomposition, is calculated.
Section
9
is devoted to the determination of the second moment about color of the matrix kinetic equation. A special case of the
SU(3_{c})
color group, is discussed. In section
10
the derivation of the equation of motion for the expected value of the colorless charge
{\mathcal{Q}}
, is considered. In section
11
the derivation of the equation of motion for the expected value of the color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
, is discussed. Nonlinear differential equations of first order for the colorless combinations of second
{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)
and third
{\mathfrak{q}}_{3}(t)
orders with respect to the mean value
\langle{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\rangle
, are derived. It is shown that for the special case of the
SU(3_{c})
color group, these two equations are completely self-consistent and their explicit analytical solutions, are obtained.

In Appendix
A
we provide the basic expressions for the effective three-plasmon vertex functions and the effective gluon propagator within the framework of the hard thermal loop approximation. In Appendix
B
, all the necessary relations and traces of a product of generators in the defining representation of the
SU(N_{c})
color group, are given. In Appendix
C
the necessary traces of a product of generators in the adjoint representation of the
SU(N_{c})
color group up to the fifth order as well as some useful relations between these generators are given.

2
Interaction Hamiltonian of plasmons and hard particles

Let us consider the application of the general Zakharov theory
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
]
to a specific system, namely to a high-temperature quark-gluon plasma in the semiclassical approximation. The gauge field potentials describing the gluon field in the system are
N_{c}\times N_{c}
matrices in the color space and are defined in terms of
A_{\mu}(x)=A_{\mu}^{a}(x)\,t^{a}
with
N^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{c}-1
Hermitian generators
t^{a}
of the color
SU(N_{c})
group in the fundamental representation
1
1

1

The color indices
a,\,b,\,c,\,\ldots
run through values
1,2,\,\ldots\,,N^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}_{c}-1
, while the vector indices
\mu,\,\nu,\,\lambda,\,\ldots
run through values
0,1,2,3
. Everywhere in this article, we imply summation over repeated indices and use the system of units with
\hbar=c=1
.
.

It is known that there exist two types of the physical soft gluon fields in an equilibrium hot quark-gluon plasma: transverse- and longitudinal-polarized ones
[
9
]
. For simplicity, we confine our analysis only to processes involving longitudinally polarized plasma excitations, which are known as
plasmons
. These excitations are a purely collective effect of the medium, which has no analogs in the conventional quantum field theory. Let us consider the gauge field potential in the form of the decomposition into plane waves
[
10
,
11
]

A^{a}_{\mu}(x)=\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\left(\frac{Z_{l}({\bf k})}{2\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}}\right)^{\!\!1/2}\!\!\left\{\epsilon^{\ \!l}_{\mu}({\bf k})\,a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}\ \!e^{-i\hskip 0.85355pt\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.56917ptt\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.56917pt\cdot\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf x}}+\epsilon^{\ast\,l}_{\mu}({\bf k})\,a^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\bf k}}\ \!e^{\hskip 0.56917pti\hskip 0.85355pt\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.56917ptt\hskip 0.85355pt-\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.56917pt\cdot\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf x}}\right\},

(2.1)

where
\epsilon^{\ \!l}_{\mu}({\bf k})
is the polarization vector of a longitudinal mode (
{\bf k}
is the wave vector). The asterisk
\ast
denotes the complex conjugation. The factor
Z_{l}({\bf k})
is the residue of the effective gluon propagator at the longitudinal pole. Finally,
\omega^{\ \!l}_{{\bf k}}
is the dispersion relation of the longitudinal mode. We consider the amplitude
a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}
for longitudinal excitations as ordinary (complex) random function. The expectation value of the product of two bosonic amplitudes is

\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pta^{\ast\hskip 0.85355pta}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\bigr\rangle=\delta^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\prime})\hskip 1.42271pt{\mathcal{N}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k},

(2.2)

where
{\mathcal{N}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
is the number density of the longitudinal plasma waves. The dispersion relation
\omega^{\ \!l}_{{\bf k}}
for plasmons satisfies the following dispersion equation
[
9
]
:

{\rm Re}\ \!\varepsilon^{l}(\omega,{\bf k})=0\ \!,

(2.3)

where

\varepsilon^{l}(\omega,{\bf k})=1+\frac{3\omega^{2}_{pl}}{{\bf k}^{\ \!2}}\biggl[1-F\biggl(\frac{\omega}{|{\bf k}|^{2}}\biggr)\biggr],\quad F(x)=\frac{x}{2}\left[\hskip 0.85355pt\ln\left|\frac{1+x}{1-x}\right|-i\pi\theta(1-|x|)\right]

is the longitudinal permittivity,
\omega_{\rm pl}^{2}=g^{2}(2N_{c}+N_{f})T^{2}/18
is a plasma frequency squared,
T
is the temperature of the system,
g
is the strong interaction constant, and
N_{f}
represents the number of flavors of massless quarks.

As it was said already above, the amplitudes
a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}
and
a^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\bf k}}
in the expansion for the longitudinal mode of oscillations (
2.1
) are usual (commuting) normal variables of the gauge field satisfying the Poisson superbracket relations

\bigl\{a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}},\,a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\bigr\}_{\rm SPB}=0,\quad\!\bigl\{a^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\bf k}},\,a^{\ast\ \!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\bigr\}_{\rm SPB}=0,\quad\!\bigl\{a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}},\,a^{\ast\ \!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\bigr\}_{\rm SPB}=\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\prime}).

(2.4)

From the other hand, in full analogy to our work
[
1
]
, we consider the amplitudes
\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}
and
\xi^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}
for
hard
momentum modes of excitations of a quark-gluon plasma as Grassmann-valued (anticommuting) variables, the Poisson superbrackets
({\rm SPB})
of which have the following standard form:

\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl\{\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}},\hskip 0.85355pt\xi^{\,j}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\!\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\}_{\rm SPB}\!=0,\quad\bigl\{\xi^{\,\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}},\hskip 0.85355pt\xi^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355ptj}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr\}_{\rm SPB}\!=0,\quad\bigl\{\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}},\hskip 0.85355pt\xi^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355ptj}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr\}_{\rm SPB}\!=\delta^{\,i\hskip 0.28436ptj}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf p}\,-\,{\bf p}^{\prime}),

(2.5)

here,
i,\,j=1,\ldots,N_{c}
. For the case of a continuous media we take the following expression as the definition of the Poisson superbracket

\bigl\{F,\,G\bigr\}_{\rm SPB}

(2.6)

=\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.56917pt\left\{\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436ptF}{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436ptG}{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,-\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436ptF}{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436ptG}{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\right\}+\int\!d{\bf p\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.56917pt\left\{\frac{\!\!\overleftarrow{\delta}\!F}{\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\!\!\overrightarrow{\delta}\!G}{\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,+\,(-1)^{P_{F}+P_{G}}\,\frac{\!\!\overrightarrow{\delta}\!F}{\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\!\!\overleftarrow{\delta}\!G}{\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\right\}.

Here,
\overrightarrow{\delta}\!/\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,*\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}}
and
\overleftarrow{\delta}\!/\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}
are the right and left functional derivatives
2
2

2

In our notations of the right and left variational derivatives we follow the notations accepted for the right and left derivatives adopted in
[
12
,
13
,
14
]
and therefore,

\delta F=\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.56917pt\left\{\frac{\delta F}{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,+\,\frac{\delta F}{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\right\}\,+\,\int\!d{\bf p\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.56917pt\left\{\frac{\!\!\overleftarrow{\delta}\!F}{\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\,+\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\,\frac{\!\!\overrightarrow{\delta}\!F}{\,\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\right\}.

,

P_{F}
and
P_{G}
designate Grassmann parity of the functions
F
and
G
, correspondingly. For simplicity of notation the abbreviation
{\rm SPB}
will be omitted, thereby suggesting that by the braces
\{\,,\}
we always mean the Poisson superbrackets.

Let us write the Hamilton equations for the functions
a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}
,
\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}
and their complex conjugation

\frac{\partial\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\partial\hskip 0.56917ptt}=-i\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\{a^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}},H\bigr\}\equiv-i\,\frac{\delta H}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pta^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\bf k}}},\qquad\frac{\partial\hskip 0.56917pta^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\partial\hskip 0.56917ptt}=-i\bigl\{a^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\bf k}},H\bigr\}\equiv i\,\frac{\delta H}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}},

(2.7)

\frac{\partial\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}}{\partial\hskip 0.56917ptt}=-i\left\{\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p},H\right\}\equiv-i\,\frac{\!\!\!\!\overrightarrow{\delta}\!H}{\,\delta\hskip 0.42677pt\xi^{\,*\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}}},\qquad\frac{\partial\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}}{\!\!\partial\hskip 0.56917ptt}=-i\left\{\xi^{\,\ast\,i}_{\bf p},H\right\}\equiv i\,\frac{\!\!\overleftarrow{\delta}\!H}{\,\delta\hskip 0.42677pt\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}}.

(2.8)

Here, the function
H
represents a Hamiltonian for the system of plasmons and hard particles, which is equal to a sum
H=H^{(0)}+H_{int}
, where

H^{(0)}=\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\ \omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}\ \!a^{\ast\hskip 0.85355pta}_{{\bf k}}a^{a}_{{\bf k}}+\int\!d{\bf p}\;\varepsilon_{\bf p}\ \!\xi^{\,\ast\hskip 1.13791pti}_{{\bf p}}\ \!\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}\

(2.9)

is the Hamiltonian of noninteracting plasmons and hard particles,
{H}_{int}
is the interaction Hamiltonian, and
\varepsilon_{\bf p}
is hard particle energy

\varepsilon_{\bf p}\simeq|{\bf p}|.

(2.10)

In the approximation of small amplitudes, the interaction Hamiltonian can be presented in the form of a formal integro-power series in the bosonic functions
{a}^{a}_{{\bf k}}
and
{a}^{\ast\hskip 0.85355pta}_{{\bf k}}
, and in the fermionic ones
\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}
and
\xi^{\,\ast\hskip 1.13791pti}_{{\bf p}}
:

H_{int}=H^{(3)}+H^{(4)}+\,\ldots\,\,,

where the third-order interaction Hamiltonian has the following structure:

\displaystyle H^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\,\Bigl\{\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{V}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,a^{\ast\hskip 0.85355pta}_{{\bf k}}\,a^{\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,a^{\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,+\,{\mathcal{V}}^{\,*\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,a^{\!\phantom{\ast}a}_{{\bf k}}\,a^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\ast\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\Bigr\}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})

(2.11)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{3}

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\,\Bigl\{\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{U}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,a^{a}_{{\bf k}}\,a^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,a^{a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,+\,{\mathcal{U}}^{\,*\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,a^{\ast\ \!a}_{{\bf k}}\,a^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,a^{\ast\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\Bigr\}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}+{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2})

\displaystyle+\!

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,d{\bf p}_{1}\,d{\bf p}_{2}\,\Bigl\{\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}^{\;a\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\;{a}^{\,a}_{{\bf k}}\,\xi^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,\xi^{\;i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,\delta({\bf k}-{\bf p}_{1}+{\bf p}_{2})+{\Phi}^{\,\ast\,a\,i_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,{a}^{\,\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}\,\xi^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,\xi^{\;i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,\delta({\bf k}+{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf p}_{2})\Bigr\}

and, correspondingly, the fourth-order interaction Hamiltonian is

\begin{split}H^{(4)}\!=&\int\!d{\bf p}\,d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\,T^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,\xi^{\,\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}\xi^{\;i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,a^{\ast\ \!\!a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}a^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\delta({\bf p}+{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}),\\[4.30554pt]
+\;\frac{1}{2}&\int\!d{\bf p}\,d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf p}_{2}\hskip 1.13791ptd{\bf p}_{3}\,T^{\,(2)\,i\,i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}\,\xi^{\,\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 1.13791pt\xi^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\xi^{\;i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,\xi^{\;i_{3}}_{{\bf p}_{3}}\,\delta({\bf p}+{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf p}_{2}-{\bf p}_{3}).\end{split}

(2.12)

As shown in
[
1
]
other possible the third-order contributions in (
2.11
) in the framework of the hard thermal loop (HTL) approximation are equal to zero. In the expression (
2.12
) the first term describes plasmon – hard-particle scattering with the resonance condition

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\bf k}+{\bf p}={\bf k}_{1}+{\bf p}_{1},\\[5.0pt]
\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}+\varepsilon_{\bf p}=\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}.\end{array}\right.

The second term is associated with the interaction of hard excitations among themselves. The expression (
2.11
) is a direct analog of the third-order interaction Hamiltonian (2.14) from the paper
[
1
]
, where to the substitutions

{\bf q}\Rightarrow{\bf p},\qquad\omega_{\bf q}^{-}\Rightarrow\varepsilon_{\bf p},\qquad{b}^{\!\phantom{\ast}\!i}_{{\bf q}}\Rightarrow{\xi}^{\!\phantom{\ast}\!b}_{{\bf p}},\qquad{b}^{\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf q}}\Rightarrow{\xi}^{\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf p}},

(2.13)

one should add substitutions of three- and four-point coefficient functions

{\mathcal{P}}^{\;a_{1}\,i\;i_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf q},\,{\bf q}_{1}}\Rightarrow{\Phi}^{\;a\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}},\quad T^{\,(2)\,i\,i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf q},\,{\bf q}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\Rightarrow T^{\,(2)\,i\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}.

(2.14)

The vertex functions
{\mathcal{V}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
satisfy the ‘‘conditions of natural symmetry’’, which specify that the integrals in Eqs. (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) are unaffected by relabeling of the dummy color indices and integration variables. These conditions have the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}={\mathcal{V}}^{\;a\,a_{2}\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}},\quad{\mathcal{U}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}={\mathcal{U}}^{\;a\,a_{2}\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}={\mathcal{U}}^{\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,a}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}},

\displaystyle T^{\,(2)\,i\,i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}=-\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,(2)\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}=-\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,(2)\,i\,i_{1}\,i_{3}\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{3},\,{\bf p}_{2}}.

The real nature of the Hamiltonian (
2.11
) is obvious. A reality of the Hamiltonian (
2.12
) entails a validity of additional relations for the vertex functions
T^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
T^{\,(2)\,i\,i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}
:

T^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=T^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,i_{1}\,i\,a_{2}\,a_{1}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}},\qquad T^{\,(2)\,i\,i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}=T^{\,(2)\,i_{2}\,i_{3}\,i\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1}}.

The information about a concrete physical system, in our case about a hot quark-gluon plasma, is contained in the dispersion law
\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}
and in the form of the interaction vertex functions in the Hamiltonians
H^{(3)}
and
H^{(4)}
. In particular, an explicit form of the three-point amplitudes
{\mathcal{V}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\;a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
within the hard thermal loop approximation was obtained in
[
15
]
. They have the following color and momentum structures:

{\mathcal{V}}^{\ \!a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{V}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}},\qquad{\mathcal{U}}^{\ \!a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{U}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}},

(2.15)

where the explicit form of the functions
{\mathcal{V}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\!
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
is written out in Appendix
A
, Eqs. (
A.1
) and (
A.2
).

3
Canonical transformations

Let us consider the transformation from the initial bosonic and fermionic variables
a^{a}_{\bf k}
and
\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}
to the new bosonic and fermionic ones
c^{a}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k}
and
\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
:

\displaystyle a^{a}_{\bf k}=a^{a}_{{\bf k}}(c^{a}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k},\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k}}\!,\,\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p},\,\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf p}}),

(3.1)

\displaystyle\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}=\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917pt(\hskip 0.56917ptc^{a}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k},\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k}}\!,\,\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p},\,\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!i}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917pt).

(3.2)

We shall demand that the Hamilton equations in terms of new functions have the form (
2.7
) and (
2.8
) with the same Hamiltonian
H
. Straightforward but rather cumbersome calculations result in two systems of integral relations. The first of them has the following form:

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436pta^{\ast\ \!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,-\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.28436pta^{\ast\ \!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\right\}+\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\ast\ \!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,+\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\ast\ \!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\right\}\!=\delta^{ab}\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}\!\ ^{\prime\prime}),

(3.3a)

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,-\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\right\}+\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,+\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{b}_{{\bf k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\right\}=0,

(3.3b)

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,-\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\right\}+\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,-\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\right\}=0,

(3.3c)

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,\ast\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,-\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta c^{\ast\ \!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\delta\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\,\ast\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!c}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}}\right\}+\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p\hskip 0.28436pt}^{\prime}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\left\{\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\xi^{\,\ast\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,-\,\frac{\overrightarrow{\delta}\!\!\hskip 1.13791pta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,\ast\ \!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\overleftarrow{\delta}\!\xi^{\,\ast\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}^{\prime\prime}}}{\delta\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!k}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}\right\}=0.

(3.3d)

The second system is written in a similar way. These canonicity conditions can be presented in a very compact form if we make use of the definition of the Poisson superbracket (
2.6
) and replace the variation variables by the new ones:
a^{a}_{\bf k}\rightarrow c^{\,a}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k}
and
\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}\rightarrow\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
. In this case the superbrackets for the original variables
a^{\,a}_{\bf k}
and
\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}
, Eqs. (
2.4
) and (
2.5
), turn to the canonicity conditions (
3.3
), which impose certain restrictions on the functional dependencies (
3.1
) and (
3.2
). Let us present the right-hand sides of (
3.1
) and (
3.2
) in the form of integro-power series in the normal variables
c^{\,a}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k}
and
\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
. The most common dependence of the transformation (
3.1
) up to cubic terms in
c^{\,a}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\bf k}
and
\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
has the following form:

a^{\,a}_{{\bf k}}=c^{\,a}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\,+

(3.4)

+\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\left[V^{(1)\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,c^{a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,+\,V^{(2)\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,c^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,+\,V^{(3)\ a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\;c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}c^{\ast\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\right]

+\int\!d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\left[F^{(1)\,a\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\;\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\zeta^{\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,+\,F^{(2)\,a\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\ \zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}+F^{(3)\,a\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\zeta^{\,\ast\hskip 1.13791pti_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\zeta^{\,\ast\hskip 1.13791pti_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\right]\hskip 17.07182pt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf p}_{2}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.85355pt

\displaystyle J^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\,a\,a_{1}\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\,c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\zeta^{\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,+\,J^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,a_{1}\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\,c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}

\displaystyle+\;

\displaystyle J^{\hskip 0.85355pt(3)\,a\,a_{1}\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\ c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,+\,J^{\hskip 0.85355pt(4)\,a\,a_{1}\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}

\displaystyle+\;

\displaystyle J^{\hskip 0.85355pt(5)\,a\,a_{1}\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,+\,J^{\hskip 0.85355pt(6)\,a\,a_{1}\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\Bigr]+\,\ldots\;\ _{.}

Similarly, the most common dependence for the transformation (
3.2
) up to cubic terms is

\xi^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}=\zeta^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}\,+

(3.5)

+\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl[\,Q^{(1)\,i\,a_{1}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\,Q^{(2)\,i\,a_{1}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\,Q^{(3)\,i\,a_{1}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\,Q^{(4)\,i\,a_{1}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,\Bigr]

\displaystyle+\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl[

\displaystyle R^{\,(1)\,i\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,c^{a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,+\,R^{\,(2)\,i\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,c^{a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle R^{\,(3)\,i\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\ast\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,+\,R^{\,(4)\,i\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,c^{a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle R^{\,(5)\,i\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,+\,R^{\,(6)\,i\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\,a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt+\!\int\!d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{3}\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl[

\displaystyle S^{\,(1)\,i\;i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p}_{3}}\,+\,S^{\,(2)\,i\;i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p}_{3}}

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle S^{\,(3)\,i\;i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p}_{3}}+S^{\,(4)\,i\;i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,\ast\,i_{3}}_{{\bf p}_{3}}\Bigr]+\,\ldots\;\ _{.}

Note first of all that the coefficient functions
V^{(1)\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
,
V^{(1)\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
,
F^{(1,3)\,a\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}
,
J^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1,3,4,6)\,a\,a_{1}\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}
,
R^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1,3,4,6)\,i\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}}
and
S^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1,2,3,4)\,i\;i_{1}\,i_{2}\,i_{3}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{3}}
must satisfy the evident conditions of natural symmetry which we don’t write out here.

Further, substituting the expansions (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) into the system of the canonicity conditions (
3.3
), we obtain rather nontrivial integral relations connecting various coefficient functions among themselves. A complete list of the integral relations connecting the coefficient functions of the second and third orders can be written out in full analogy with the corresponding relations from the paper
[
1
]
. These integral relations will not be needed in the present work, so we will not give them. Here, we provide only algebraic relations for the second-order coefficient functions:

\begin{split}&V^{(2)\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.28436pt2\hskip 0.85355ptV^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\,a_{2}\,a_{1}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}},\quad V^{(3)\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=V^{(3)\,a_{1}\,a\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2}},\\[4.30554pt]
&Q^{(1)\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.28436ptF^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,i_{2}\,i_{1}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1}},\qquad Q^{(2)\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{2}}=2\hskip 0.28436ptF^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\,a\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}},\\[4.30554pt]
&Q^{(3)\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{2}}=F^{(2)\,a\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}},\qquad\quad\,Q^{(4)\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{2}}=2\hskip 0.28436ptF^{(3)\,a\,i_{1}\,i_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}.\end{split}

(3.6)

4
Eliminating ‘‘non-essential ’’ Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
. Effective fourth-order Hamiltonian

The next step in the construction of an effective theory is the procedure of eliminating the third-order interaction Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
, Eq. (
2.11
), upon switching from the original bosonic and fermionic functions
a^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\bf k}
and
\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}
to the new functions
c^{\,a}_{\bf k}
and
\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
as a result of the canonical transformations (
3.4
) and (
3.5
). This elimination procedure is presented in detail in
[
1
]
, so here we only give a brief description of the procedure and its final result, which follows from expressions (4.3) of
[
1
]
, with appropriate substitutions (
2.13
) and (
2.14
).

To achieve eliminating the third-order interaction Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
, we substitute the expansions (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) into the free-field Hamiltonian
H^{(0)}
, Eq. (
2.9
), and keep only the terms cubic in
c^{\,a}_{{\bf k}}
and
\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
. Then in the Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
, Eq. (
2.11
), we perform the replacements:
a^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\bf k}\rightarrow c^{\,a}_{\bf k}
and
\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}\rightarrow\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
. Adding the expression thus obtained to that which follows from the free-field Hamiltonian
H^{(0)}
, collecting similar terms and using the relations (
3.6
), finally we obtain an explicit form of the coefficient functions in the quadratic part of the canonical transformations (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) that exclude the cubic terms in the interaction Hamiltonian:

\left\{\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}&V^{(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.56917pt\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}}-\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{2}}}\,\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}),\\[12.91663pt]
&V^{(3)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.56917pt\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{U}}^{\hskip 0.85355pt*\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}}+\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}+\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{2}}}\,\delta({\bf k}+{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2}),\end{array}\ \right.

(4.1)

\left\{\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}&F^{(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{1}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,=\,F^{(3)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{1}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,=\,0,\\[12.91663pt]
&F^{(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{1}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1}}=-\hskip 0.56917pt\displaystyle\frac{\Phi^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}\,i}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}}}{\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}}\,\delta({\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1}+{\bf p}).\end{array}\ \right.

The coefficients
V^{(2)}
and
Q^{(n)},\,n=1,\,2,\,3,\,4
are found from Eq. (
3.6
). We have previously obtained the relations (
4.1
) in
[
15
]
. These expressions imply that due to specific character of the dispersion equations for soft bosonic excitations (
2.3
) and for hard mode excitations (
2.10
) in the hot quark-gluon plasma, the resonance conditions for three-wave processes with plasmons and for Cherenkov radiation (or absorption) of plasmons by a hard particle

\vskip-8.5359pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf k}={\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2},\\[6.45831pt]
\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}}=\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}+\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{2}},\end{array}\right.\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf k}+{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2}=0,\\[6.45831pt]
\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}}+\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}+\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{2}}=0,\end{array}\ \right.\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf p}={\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1},\\[6.45831pt]
\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}=\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}-\omega^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\end{array}\ \right.

have no solutions. In other words, the processes of emission or absorption of collective excitation by another collective excitation and by a hard particle that lie on the mass shells
\omega=\omega^{\,l}_{\bf k}
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\bf p}
are forbidden.

Next we write out an explicit form of the effective fourth-order Hamiltonian, which describes the elastic scattering of plasmon off hard particle. In terms of the original variables
a^{a}_{\bf k}
and
\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}
, the Hamiltonian for the scattering process is defined by the first term on the right-hand side of (
2.12
). In this term we make the substitution
a^{\,a}_{\bf k}\rightarrow c^{\,a}_{\bf k}
and
\xi^{\,i}_{\bf p}\rightarrow\zeta^{\,i}_{\bf p}
. Further we define all similar terms of fourth-order product
c^{\,\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\;a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\zeta}^{\ast\ \!\!i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\zeta}^{\!\phantom{\ast}\!i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}
from the free-field Hamiltonian
H^{(0)}
, Eq. (
2.9
), and from the Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
, Eq. (
2.11
), to be arisen under the canonical transformations (
3.4
) and (
3.5
). Putting the pieces together, we result in the effective fourth-order Hamiltonian describing the elastic scattering process of plasmon off a hard color particle:

{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\hskip 0.56917pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.56917ptg\hskip 0.56917ptG}=\int\!\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\,\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}\zeta^{\;i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}c^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\delta({\bf p}+{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\,d{\bf p}\,d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2},

(4.2)

where the
complete effective amplitude

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
has the following structure:

\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=T^{\,(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\vskip-8.5359pt

(4.3)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{2}\,\Biggl[\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}-{\bf k}_{2}}}\,+\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}^{\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355ptj}_{{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}-{\bf k}_{2}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptj}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}}

\displaystyle-\,\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf k}_{2}+\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf k}_{1}+\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}^{\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptj\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{2}+{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptj\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf p},\,{\bf p}}\Biggr]

\displaystyle+\,\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}\,-\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf p}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{V}}^{\;a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{{\bf k}_{1},{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}-{{\bf k}_{2}}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}}

\displaystyle+\,\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}}\,-\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf p}-{\bf p}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}^{\,a\,i\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}-{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,{\mathcal{V}}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{{\bf k}_{2},{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}.

Hereinafter, the effective Hamiltonians will be designated by the calligraphic letter
{\mathcal{H}}
, including also the Hamiltonian
{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)}
for non-interacting plasmons and hard particles in the new variables:

{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)}=\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\;\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}\ \!c^{\ast\hskip 0.85355pta}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}}+\int\!d{\bf p}\;\varepsilon^{\phantom{b}}_{\bf p}\hskip 1.13791pt\zeta^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.28436pt\zeta^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}.

5
Fourth-order correlation function for soft and hard excitations

Let us consider the construction of a system of kinetic equations describing the elastic scattering process of plasmon off a hard particle. As the interaction Hamiltonian here, we take the effective Hamiltonian
{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{gG\rightarrow gG}
, Eq. (
4.2
). The equations of motion for the fermionic
\zeta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.85355pt\prime}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}},\,\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}}
and bosonic
c^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}},\,c^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}
normal variables are defined by the corresponding Hamilton equations. For the hard particle excitations we have

\frac{\partial\hskip 1.13791pt\zeta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.85355pt\prime}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}=-\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 1.13791pt\Bigl\{\zeta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355pt,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)\!}+{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\rightarrow g\hskip 0.56917ptG}\Bigr\}=-\hskip 0.56917pti\hskip 1.13791pt\varepsilon^{\,\phantom{i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\,\zeta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}

(5.1)

-\;i\!\int\!\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\,i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf p}^{\prime},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\;i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}+{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\,d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}_{2},

\hskip 8.5359pt\frac{\partial\hskip 1.13791pt\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}=-\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 1.13791pt\Bigl\{\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.85355pt,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)\!}+{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\rightarrow g\hskip 0.56917ptG}\Bigr\}=i\hskip 1.13791pt\varepsilon^{\phantom{\ast i}\!\!}_{{\bf p}}\,\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}_{{\bf p}}

(5.2)

\hskip 17.07182pt+\;i\!\int\!\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,i\,i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\ast\ \!\!a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\delta({\bf p}+{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\,d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}_{2}.

In the latter equation we have taken into account the symmetry condition for the complete scattering amplitude

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,i_{1}\,i\,a_{2}\,a_{1}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p},\;{\bf k}_{2},\ {\bf k}_{1}}.

(5.3)

This relation is a consequence of the requirement of the reality of the effective Hamiltonian
{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\rightarrow g\hskip 0.56917ptG}
. Further, for soft Bose-excitations we define the second pair of the canonical equations of motions with the same Hamiltonian

\frac{\partial\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!\!a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}=-\hskip 1.13791pti\hskip 1.13791pt\Bigl\{c^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!\!a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355pt,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)\!}+{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\rightarrow g\hskip 0.56917ptG}\Bigr\}=-i\hskip 1.13791pt\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,c^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!\!a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}}

(5.4)

-\;i\!\int\!\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)\,i_{1}\,i_{2}\,a^{\prime}\;a_{1}}_{\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\;i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}+{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{2})\,d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}_{1},

\hskip 8.5359pt\frac{\partial\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}=-\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 1.13791pt\Bigl\{c^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)\!}+{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\rightarrow g\hskip 0.56917ptG}\Bigr\}=i\hskip 1.13791pt\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}}\,c^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}

(5.5)

\hskip 14.22636pt-\;i\!\int\!\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}\,i_{2}\,a\,a_{1}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\;{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\;i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i_{2}}_{{\bf p}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\,\delta({\bf k}+{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{2})\,d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}_{1}.

In the case when an external gauge field is absent in the system, the exact equations (
5.1
), (
5.2
), (
5.4
), and (
5.5
) enable us to define the kinetic equations for the hard particle number density
{\mathfrak{n}}^{i\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}_{{\bf p}}
and for the plasmon number density
{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}}_{\bf k}
. If the ensemble of interacting Bose-excitations at low nonlinearity level has random phases, then it can be statistically described by introducing the bosonic correlation function
[
15
]
:

\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\ast\ \!\!a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!a^{\prime}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\prime})\hskip 1.13791pt{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a\hskip 0.56917pta^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}}.

(5.6)

However, now we do not consider the spectral density
{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a\hskip 0.56917pta^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}}
to have a trivial diagonal structure in an effective color space (see Eq. (
2.2
)) as was the case in the previous paper
[
15
]
. The color decomposition of
{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a\hskip 0.56917pta^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}}
will be presented below.

For hard momentum modes of quark-gluon plasma excitations, we make use an ansatz separating the color and momentum degrees of freedom, namely we assume that

\zeta^{\,i}_{{\bf p}}=\theta^{\,i}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta_{{\bf p}},\qquad\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}=\theta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{{\bf p}}.

(5.7)

Here we have introduced a set of the Grassmann-valued color charges
\theta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\ \!\!i}
and
\theta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i}
belonging to the defining representation of the
SU(N_{c})
Lie algebra
[
17
,
16
]
. These color charges are in involution with respect to the conjugation operation
\ast
. The complex function
\zeta_{{\bf p}}
is an usual commutative random function of the momentum variable
{\bf p}
. In the representation (
5.7
) we have a complete decoupling of the color and momentum degrees of freedom. This is true only if we neglect the influence of soft collective excitations of the gauge field on the change of the momentum of a hard particle, i.e. the momentum of the particle is fixed and all interaction is carried out only through the color degree of freedom. For determination of the desired kinetic equations, it is necessary first to perform calculations exactly, without using any approximation. Only at the end of all calculations we must take into account the fact that the momentum of hard particles is much greater than the momentum of soft plasma excitations, i.e.,

|{\bf p}_{1}|,\,|{\bf p}_{2}|\gg|{\bf k}|,\,|{\bf k}_{1}|,

and perform the corresponding approximations of the derived expressions. By virtue of the decomposition (
5.7
), we can represent also the hard mode correlation function in the factorized form

\bigl\langle\,\!\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}\zeta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=\bigl\langle\,\!\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}}\hskip 1.13791pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}^{\prime}\!}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\,\!\theta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}\hskip 0.56917pt\theta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,

where, in turn, we believe

\bigl\langle\,\!\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}}\hskip 1.13791pt\zeta^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr\rangle=\delta({\bf p}-{\bf p}^{\prime})\hskip 1.13791ptn_{\bf p}.

Thus, in full analogy with (
5.6
) we can write

\!\!\bigl\langle\,\!\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}\zeta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=\delta({\bf p}-{\bf p}^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{n}}^{i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.85355pti}_{\bf p},

where we have introduced the matrix function
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}}
setting by the definition

{\mathfrak{n}}^{\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\hskip 0.56917ptn_{\bf p}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\theta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\ \!\!i}\hskip 1.13791pt\theta^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

We draw your attention to the arrangement of color indices on the left- and right-hand sides of the previous expression.

Let us derive the kinetic equations for the number densities of hard excitations
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}}
and plasmons
{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}}
employing the Hamilton equations (
5.1
), (
5.2
), (
5.4
) and (
5.5
). Using precisely the same reasoning as in
paper
[
1
]
, we obtain matrix analog of the equations (10.7) and (10.8) in the above-mentioned work

\delta({\bf p}-{\bf p}\!\ ^{\prime})\,\frac{\partial\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{n}}^{\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.56917pti}_{{\bf p}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}\ =-\hskip 0.85355pti\!\int\!d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}_{2}\,\times

(5.8)

\times\hskip 0.56917pt\biggl\{\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\,{\bf p}^{\prime},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,I^{\,i\,i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}+{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})-\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,I^{\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\,\delta({\bf p}+{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\biggr\}

and

\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}\!\ ^{\prime})\,\frac{\partial\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}\ =-\hskip 0.85355pti\!\int\!d{\bf p}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd{\bf p}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}_{1}\,\times

(5.9)

\times\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl\{\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)\,i_{1}\,i_{2}\,a^{\prime}\,a_{1}}_{\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\,I^{\;i_{1}\,i_{2}\,a\,a_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\,\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}+{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{2})-\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,i_{1}\,i_{2}\,a\,a_{1}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\,I^{\;i_{2}\,i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a^{\prime}}_{{\bf p}_{2},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\delta({\bf k}+{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf p}_{2})\biggr\},

where

I^{\,i\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}\,\zeta^{\,i_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\,\bigr\rangle

is the four-point correlation function. By differentiating the correlation function
I^{\,i\ i_{1}\ a_{1}\ a_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
with respect to
t
with allowance made for (
5.1
), (
5.2
), (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), we derive the equation the right-hand side of which contains the six-order correlation functions of the variables
\zeta^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,i}_{{\bf p}}\!,\,\zeta^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!\!i}_{{\bf p}}
and
c^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!\!a}_{{\bf k}},\,c^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}
:

\frac{\partial\hskip 0.28436ptI^{\,i\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}=i\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl[\,\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}+\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\bigr]\,I^{\,i\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\ +

(5.10)

\displaystyle+\;i\!

\displaystyle\int\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,i\;i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}\,a^{\prime}_{1}\,a^{\prime}_{2}}_{\ {\bf p},\,{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}}\,\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\zeta^{\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}}\,c^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}\,\bigr\rangle\,\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}+{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}-{\bf p}-{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1})\,d{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle-\;i\!

\displaystyle\int\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)\,i_{1}\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}\,a^{\prime}_{1}\,a^{\prime}_{2}}_{\ {\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}}\,\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}}\zeta^{\;i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}\,\bigr\rangle\,\delta({\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}+{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2})\,d{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle+\;i\!

\displaystyle\int\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}\,a^{\prime}_{1}}_{\ {\bf p}^{\prime}_{2},\,{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}\!\!}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\phantom{\hskip 0.56917pt\ast}\!i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}\,\bigr\rangle\,\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}+{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}-{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1})\,d{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 1.13791ptd{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle-\;i\!

\displaystyle\int\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}\;a^{\prime}_{1}}_{\ {\bf p}^{\prime}_{1},\,{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}\!\!}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\bigr\rangle\,\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}+{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}-{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}-{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1})\,d{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 1.13791ptd{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}.

As in the pure fermionic case
[
1
]
, we close the chain of equations by expressing the six-order correlation functions in terms of the pair correlation functions. We keep only those terms that give the proper contributions to the required kinetic equations:

\begin{split}&\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\!\zeta^{\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}\bigr\rangle\,\simeq\,\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1})\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1})\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}-{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2})\,{\mathfrak{n}}^{i^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}a^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}},\\[6.45831pt]
&\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}}\zeta^{\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}\,\bigr\rangle\simeq\,\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf p})\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2})\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}_{2}-{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1})\,{\mathfrak{n}}^{i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}i^{\phantom{\prime}}}_{{\bf p}}{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}a^{\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}},\\[6.45831pt]
&\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}\!\!}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\simeq-\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}-{\bf p})\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1})\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2})\,{\mathfrak{n}}^{i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}i}_{{\bf p}}\,{\mathfrak{n}}^{i_{1}i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}},\\[6.45831pt]
&\bigl\langle\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}\!\!}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\,i^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\,\zeta^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast\,i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta^{\phantom{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}\!i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}}_{{\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\simeq-\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{2}-{\bf p})\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf p}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf p}_{1})\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}^{\prime}_{1}-{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1})\,{\mathfrak{n}}^{i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}i}_{{\bf p}}\,{\mathfrak{n}}^{i_{1}i^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf p}_{1}}{\mathcal{N}}^{\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}a^{\prime}_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}.\end{split}

(5.11)

In the interaction Hamiltonian (
2.11
) we set for the three-point vertex function
{\Phi}^{\;a\,i_{1}\;i_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}
:

{\Phi}^{\;a\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}=(t^{\hskip 0.85355pta})^{\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{2}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{2}}.

Then, by taking into account the representation (
2.15
) for the vertex function
{\mathcal{V}}^{\ \!a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
the color structure of the complete effective amplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, Eq. (
4.3
), looks like

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=[\,t^{a_{1}}\!,\hskip 0.85355ptt^{a_{2}}]^{\,i\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}}\,\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\{t^{a_{1}}\!,\hskip 0.85355ptt^{a_{2}}\}^{\,i\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}}\,\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}},

(5.12)

where the
effective subamplitudes

\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}
and
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}
have the following structures:

\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=T^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

(5.13)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{4}\,\Biggl[\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf k}_{2}+\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf k}_{1}+\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}_{{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{2}+{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf p},\,{\bf p}}

\displaystyle+\,\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}-{\bf k}_{2}}}\,+\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}_{{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}-{\bf k}_{2}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}}\Biggr]

\displaystyle-\,i\hskip 0.85355pt\Biggl[\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}\,-\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf p}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{V}}_{{\bf k}_{1},{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}-{{\bf k}_{2}}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}}

\displaystyle-\,\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}}\,-\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf p}-{\bf p}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}_{{\bf p}-{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,{\mathcal{V}}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{{\bf k}_{2},{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\Biggr],

\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=T^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\vskip-8.5359pt

(5.14)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{4}\,\Biggl[\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf k}_{2}+\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf k}_{1}+\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}_{{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{2}+{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf p},\,{\bf p}}

\displaystyle-\,\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}-{\bf k}_{2}}}\,+\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}+\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.56917pt{\Phi}_{{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf p}-{\bf k}_{2}}\,{\Phi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p}_{1}-{\bf k}_{1}}\Biggl].

We should have put the imaginary unit
i
before the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.12
), but we didn’t do that. From the decomposition (
5.12
) and the realness condition (
5.3
) the symmetry properties for the effective subamplitudes
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}
and
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}
follow:

\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}},\qquad\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=\mathscr{T}^{\,\ast\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{\;{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf p},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}.

(5.15)

In section
7
we show that in the limit
|{\bf p}|,\,|{\bf p}_{1}|\gg|{\bf k}_{1}|,\,|{\bf k}_{2}|
the following approximate equality for the effective subamplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
will be true

\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{S}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\simeq 0,

so that in the future in the color decomposition (
5.12
) we leave only the contribution with subamplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, i.e., we set

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,\simeq\,i\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,e}\hskip 0.56917pt(\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,e\hskip 0.56917pt})^{\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}}\,\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\equiv-\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,e\hskip 0.56917pt}\bigr)^{a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt(\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,e\hskip 0.56917pt})^{\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}^{\,(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}},

(5.16)

where
\bigl(T^{\,a}\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\equiv-i\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}
.
For convenience of further considerations, let us also write out an expression for the conjugate amplitude:

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pti\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\simeq\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,e\hskip 0.56917pt}\bigr)^{a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt(\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,e\hskip 0.56917pt})^{\hskip 0.85355pti_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti}\,\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2,\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{A}})}_{\;{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}.

(5.17)

Substituting the expressions (
5.11
), (

) and (
5.17
) into the right-hand side of (
5.10
) and considering the symmetry condition (
5.3
) for the scattering amplitude, instead of (
5.10
) we derive the equation for the fourth-order correlation function

\frac{\partial\hskip 0.28436ptI^{\,i\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}}{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}=i\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl[\,\varepsilon_{{\bf p}}+\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-\varepsilon_{{\bf p}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\bigr]\,I^{\,i\;i_{1}\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{{\bf p},\,{\bf p}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

(5.18)
